# NEEDLE: Which search API moves the needle for AI agents?

By Andrey Styskin, Matthias Petri and Ilya Gusev, [Keenable](https://keenable.ai/).

Last run: Oct 31, 23:50 UTC. News runs each hour; all other suites run each day. Times are UTC.

As of Oct 31, 2026, NEEDLE compares 24 web search APIs on the same queries in five verticals. [Keenable](https://keenable.ai/) leads overall with 71% of the ceiling (Perplexity 69%, Perplexity Fast 68%, Exa and Parallel 67%, intervals overlap), and leads Scholar (76%; context.dev 75%, intervals overlap), AgenticRare (50%; Perplexity 48%, intervals overlap) and Legal (76%; Perplexity Fast 75%, intervals overlap). Exa Lite leads News (65%; Exa 65%, intervals overlap), where Keenable ties for fifth (63%); Exa Lite leads Finance (95%; Parallel 94%, intervals overlap), where Keenable is seventh (90%). Keenable's [Search API](https://docs.keenable.ai/) has the lowest median latency, 143 ms, at $1.00 per 1,000 queries.

NEEDLE (**N**ews, **E**veryday, **E**xpert, **D**eep-tail, and **L**egal **E**valuation) is a live open-source search benchmark. It compares search engines with queries that model agent behavior. The five verticals match the use cases we see each day in Keenable's own search traffic:

- **Scholar:** expert literature searches: find a specific paper from a degraded title, a full-text detail, or a tip-of-the-tongue description
- **News:** breaking and developing stories; new queries each hour from curated RSS feeds and Google Trends
- **Finance:** everyday company and SEC-filing lookups with known answers from public registries
- **AgenticRare:** obscure, long-tail entities; rare-word queries sampled from the [DeepResearchGym](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3805712.3809627), [OpenResearcher](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20278), and [LRAT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04949) agentic search logs
- **Legal:** find a specific court opinion or section of the Code of Federal Regulations

**Methodology.** Each engine in a run receives the same query text. We score each engine on the ranking, titles, and snippets it returned. We do not fetch pages and we do not re-rank. The judge does not see the engine name. We make new query sets from public sources, News each hour and the rest each day, so there is no fixed set to overfit. Appendix C gives the full comparison protocol. **The benchmark is open source: the [keenableai/needle](https://github.com/keenableai/needle) repo is public, every run executes in plain GitHub Actions, and we welcome contributions.**

**Metrics.** News and AgenticRare queries have no single correct result, so we measure ranking quality. An LLM judge rates each result from 0 to 4. We report nDCG@5, normalized so that the pooled *ultimate* engine scores exactly 1, with a penalty for duplicate URLs. Finance measures if the answer text reaches the agent: we scan the top-5 titles and snippets and report answer-recall@5 with MRR@5. Scholar and Legal are known-item tasks: we match identifiers and report recall@10 and recall@5 with MRR. Appendix D gives the exact definitions.

**The *ultimate* ceiling.** The italicized row in each leaderboard is *ultimate*, a synthetic engine. It pools all engines' results per query and ranks them by their relevance labels (Appendix C). The standings radar plots each engine as a share of *ultimate*.

## Index independence

Repeated overlap on junk or near-identical lists suggests a shared upstream index (all benchmarks).

### Definition: suspicious sharing

the pairwise test behind the matrix and the borrowing graph (src/needle/shared/overlap.py)

Let U_A(q) be the normalized URLs engine A returned for query q, and L_A(q) ⊆ U_A(q) those judged below HM. Engines A and B *share suspiciously* on q when

|L_A(q) ∩ L_B(q)| ≥ 1 or |U_A(q) ∩ U_B(q)| ≥ 3.

Each matrix cell shows the share of comparable queries (both searches succeeded and at least one returned a result) where the pair shares suspiciously, pooled over the selected runs. Engines that share *good* results show normal ranking behavior. Engines that share junk, or 3+ URLs at once, possibly share an upstream index. Results on the known-answer verticals have no judgements, so only the second clause can apply.

### Index independence — four views

suspicious-sharing matrix, URL-intersection matrix, borrowing graph, and per-engine result uniqueness

#### Suspicious sharing (all runs)

share of queries where the pair shares an irrelevant URL or 3+ URLs of any rating

## Appendix — methodology & configuration

We pin everything below in [keenableai/needle](https://github.com/keenableai/needle); you can compute each number on this page again from the archived artifacts (Appendix G).

### Appendix A — judgement browser

pick a benchmark, then a run, to inspect each query; full runs on the [HF dataset](https://huggingface.co/datasets/keenable-ai/needle-results)

### Appendix B — query generation

how we build each slice's queries (per-bench generate.py); the exact rows every run used are archived (Appendix G)

We make each query set fresh from public sources: News each hour, the other verticals each day.

For Scholar we sample papers from arXiv (computer, physical, life, and social sciences) and Europe PMC (health sciences). Four age bands reach from the last week back to approximately five years. Each paper gives one query per slice, so the four Scholar slices rank engines on the same papers. The gaps between the slices are the point: title vs body isolates full-text indexing, body vs clue isolates keyword-vs-prose handling, and tot isolates semantic retrieval.

We leak-check each LLM-written Scholar query: at least two content words must not appear in the title or the abstract. We keep a paper only if each slice produced a valid query. The known-item and tip-of-the-tongue framing follows Exa's ["SOTA Search Over Academic Publications"](https://exa.ai/blog/publications-search) (Jul 2026).

The AgenticRare stream merges search queries from three public agent-trajectory releases: the anonymized DeepResearchGym logs (14M+ search requests from real agentic clients; ["Agentic Search in the Wild"](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3805712.3809627), SIGIR '26), the [OpenResearcher](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20278) deep-research trajectories (TIGER-AI-Lab, 2026), and [LRAT-Train](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04949) ("Learning to Retrieve from Agent Trajectories", SIGIR '26).

| vertical | slice | query construction |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Scholar | title | the paper's title, lowercased with LaTeX and punctuation stripped; plain known-item lookup, answerable from metadata |
| Scholar | body | a keyword query (3–8 words) anchored on a globally distinctive detail that appears in the full text but not in the title or abstract (a named method, cohort, instrument, or an unusual measured value); answerable only by engines that index the paper body |
| Scholar | clue | a natural-language question built from 2–3 precise factual clues taken from the body, the way a researcher re-finds a paper from remembered specifics |
| Scholar | tot | a hedged, half-remembered description with every specific blurred: approximate quantities, no names the paper introduces, at most two content words reused from the title |
| News | fresh | the newest item per feed from ~124 curated RSS feeds within a freshness window, merged with Google Trends RSS across all 52 US geos (fuzzy-deduped, capped by traffic); an LLM projects each item into a keyword query and refuses evergreen content |
| Finance | finance / finance_nl | registry facts per company (CEO, founding year, HQ country, website, employees, LEI) from Wikidata and GLEIF, ticker from SEC, asked as a keyword query and, where a template exists, as a natural-language variant |
| Finance | filings | single-quarter 10-Q facts (net income, operating income, diluted EPS) from SEC XBRL, companies stratified by market cap; half the queries are quoted phrases |
| Finance | filingdoc | known-item SEC-filing retrieval; the gold answer is the filing's accession number; half the queries carry operator syntax (site:, "quoted", after:/before:) |
| AgenticRare | rare-entity | sampled from the merged DeepResearchGym + OpenResearcher + LRAT-Train query stream, keeping queries where BERT WordPiece splits a word into 5+ subwords or maps it to [UNK]; fastText language ID gates for English, an n-gram cap drops near-copies, and each run samples stratified by query length |
| Legal | caselaw | recent published opinions from the CourtListener API across 14 federal courts, one caption-style query per case; gold is the reporter citation, docket number with party tokens, and cluster id |
| Legal | code | eCFR sections; an LLM projects each section into a query about its regulatory substance with one verbatim quoted span, rejected if it leaks the citation; gold is the "title CFR section" citation |

### Appendix C — comparison protocol

the fairness rules behind every number on this page

We send each engine in a run the same query text. We pin the per-engine request shapes in git (Appendix F). The runner sends requests in sequence, one call at a time across the whole run. Thus the latency percentiles are comparable across engines, and no engine gets concurrent load. Engines see the live web minutes apart in a run, not at the same instant.

We judge an engine on its own ranking, titles, and snippets. We do not fetch pages and we do not re-rank. We cap the evidence at the same snippet length for each engine, so engines that return more content get no extra signal to the judge or the answer matchers. We judge each distinct combination of URL, title, snippet, and date once per query. We use that rating again where the identical result occurs for other engines (Appendix E gives the full judging setup).

A failed search scores zero when the engine's side caused it (timeout, server error, content filter, a rejected query); an agent gets nothing back either. We remove a failed search from the mean only when our own account or plan caused it (HTTP 401, 402, 403 or 429, a plan limit, or a message about our credits, quota, billing or API key). We pace each engine to its rate limit and retry a 429 with exponential backoff. We also remove failed judgements. Reports list `search_errors`, split into `account_errors` and `service_errors`, and `judge_errors`.

We make fresh News queries each hour and new known-answer queries each day from public sources (Appendix B), so there is no fixed benchmark set to overfit. We archive the exact queries with each run (Appendix G).

Each vertical also reports *ultimate*, a synthetic engine. It pools all engines' results per query and ranks them oracle-style: best judge rating first on the judged verticals, and the gold item first on the known-answer verticals. Its score shows what one engine could get if it always returned the best results that the full field found. This separates "the engine ranked poorly" from "no engine found better results". We do not include *ultimate* in the overlap and uniqueness statistics.

### Appendix D — metric definitions

src/needle/shared/metrics.py and per-bench score.py

| metric | benches | definition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| recall@10, MRR@10 | Scholar | known-item: the gold paper identified in the top 10 by arXiv ID / DOI / PMID match. |
| nDCG@5 | News, AgenticRare | DCG over the top 5 results (Σ gain(rᵢ) / log₂(i + 1), with judge ratings mapped to gain 4 → 1.0, 3 → 0.667, 2 → 0.117, 1/0 → 0), divided by the DCG of the ideal ranking: the pooled ultimate ordering of every engine's deduplicated results, so ultimate scores exactly 1. A query is scored only if every one of its results was judged (otherwise it is dropped from the mean and counted as judge errors) and only if the pooled ideal has any gain; queries where no engine found anything relevant are excluded. |
| redundancy penalty | News, AgenticRare | applied to engine ratings before scoring: a duplicate URL drops the rating to 0. The pooled ideal is already deduplicated, so no penalty applies there. Skipped when the query itself carries a site: operator. Raw and penalized ratings are both shown in the judgement browser. |
| answer-recall@5, MRR@5 | Finance | a query is a hit when the gold answer appears in a top-5 title or snippet (deterministic matcher: a 0.1% band for money, with bare table figures in thousands or millions and parenthesized losses; an LLM-judge backstop for paraphrased non-money answers). MRR is the mean reciprocal rank of the first hit. |
| recall@5, MRR@5 | Legal | known-item: the gold opinion or CFR section identified by citation, docket (with a party-token check), CFR citation, or URL match. |
| latency p50 / p95 | all | percentiles over per-call durations of successful search API calls, pooled across queries in the window; runs published without per-call samples contribute their per-run percentiles instead. Failed calls carry no latency sample and show up in the error rate. |

### Appendix E — LLM judge

rates News and AgenticRare results; Finance fallback matcher only; Scholar and Legal are deterministic

We judge with `openai/gpt-6-luna` via OpenRouter, high reasoning effort, temperature 0, one pointwise call per document. We record the model for each run as `judge_model` in its archived report. The judgement browser shows it when you select a run, so judge changes stay visible in the history.

The judge does not see the engine identity. The prompt contains the query, the document title, the URL, the published date, and the snippet — not the engine name. We judge each distinct combination of URL, title, snippet, and date once per query and use that rating again where the identical result occurs for other engines. We judge the same URL with a different snippet separately, with the same blind judge and rubric.

A judge preference for a snippet style would be a shared bias across engines. We publish the per-result reasoning with each run, so you can audit this.

Rubric: 0–4 Needs Met scale (FailsM / SM / HM / FullyM) with hard caps for a wrong entity, stale coverage of dated events, and search-operator compliance ("quoted phrases", site:). The full prompt is one template in the repo: src/needle/shared/prompts/judgement.jinja.

Known limitation: we operate NEEDLE ourselves, and we did not yet do a systematic human-agreement audit of the judge. But you can check the numbers: the judge is blind and shared across engines, each rating includes its reasoning, and we publish the queries, prompts, and raw results. Thus anyone can score any rating again.

### Appendix F — engine configuration

request shapes pinned in git (src/needle/shared/search/); k = 5 results per query except Scholar (10). Engines are live, unversioned APIs; provider-side changes surface as trend shifts.

Every run builds each client with the same snippet budget of 2,000 characters. Keenable, Exa and Ceramic accept a request-side length parameter, so they get it in the request (snippet_max_length=2000, highlights.maxCharacters=2000, maxDescriptionLength=2000). The other engines have no such parameter; we clip whatever they return to the same 2,000 characters before judging and answer matching (Appendix C). Requests time out at 30 s (60 s for Perplexity, Parallel and Jina, 180 s for claude-search and chatgpt-search) and retry up to 3 times with exponential backoff on transport errors, 429, and 5xx.

| engine | API | pinned request |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Keenable | Keenable POST /v1/search | mode=realtime, snippet_max_length=2000; query verbatim with site: kept in the text, after:/before: → published_after:/published_before: tokens; no count parameter — we keep the first k results |
| google/serper | Serper.dev (Google SERP) POST /search | q = query verbatim (operators kept as text), num=min(k,100), gl=us, hl=en; knowledge-graph and answer-box entries fold into the ranking |
| bing/searchapi | SearchApi.io GET /search | engine=bing, q = query verbatim (operators kept as text), num=min(k,50); knowledge-graph and answer-box fold in |
| brave | Brave GET /web/search | q = query text with site: kept, clipped to 75 words / 600 chars; count=min(k,20), country=us, search_lang=en, result_filter=web, text_decorations=false; after:/before: → freshness=YYYY-MM-DDtoYYYY-MM-DD; extra_snippets joined with newlines, falling back to description when absent |
| brave-llmcontext | Brave POST /llm/context | q = query text clipped to 75 words / 600 chars; country=us, search_lang=en; after:/before: → freshness=YYYY-MM-DDtoYYYY-MM-DD; per-URL snippets joined with newlines and clipped client-side to the 2,000-char budget, published date from the sources map |
| exa | Exa POST /search | type=auto, numResults=k, contents.highlights.maxCharacters=2000; site: → includeDomains, after:/before: → startPublishedDate/endPublishedDate (full-day UTC bounds) |
| exa-lite | Exa POST /search | same as exa but type=lite |
| exa-instant | Exa POST /search | same as exa but type=instant |
| parallel | Parallel POST /v1/search | mode=basic, search_queries=[query text], advanced_settings.max_results=k; site: → source_policy.include_domains, after: → source_policy.after_date (before: has no mapping and is dropped) |
| parallel-turbo | Parallel POST /v1/search | same as parallel but mode=turbo |
| perplexity | Perplexity POST /search | max_results=min(k,20), search_context_size=low; site: → search_domain_filter, after:/before: → search_after_date_filter/search_before_date_filter (MM/DD/YYYY) |
| perplexity-fast | Perplexity POST /search | same as perplexity but search_type=fast (perplexity leaves the field unset, so it takes the API default web) |
| tavily | Tavily POST /search | search_depth=basic, max_results=min(k,20); site: → include_domains, after:/before: → start_date/end_date (ISO dates) |
| linkup | Linkup POST /v1/search | depth=standard, outputType=searchResults, maxResults=k; site: → includeDomains, after:/before: → fromDate/toDate (ISO dates); HTML entities in name/content unescaped, image results dropped; the API returns no published date |
| tinyfish | TinyFish GET api.search.tinyfish.ai | query = query text clipped to 2,000 chars; no count parameter — we keep the first k results; site: → include_domains (comma-separated), after:/before: → after_date/before_date (ISO dates) |
| you | You.com POST /v1/search | count=min(k,100); site: → include_domains, after:/before: → freshness=YYYY-MM-DDtoYYYY-MM-DD; web and news sections merge, deduplicated by URL |
| firecrawl | Firecrawl POST /v2/search | limit=min(k,100), sources=[web]; site: kept in the query text, after:/before: → tbs=cdr:1,cd_min:M/D/YYYY,cd_max:M/D/YYYY |
| kagi | Kagi POST /api/v1/search | query = query text, limit=min(k,1024); site: → lens.sites_included, after:/before: → filters.after/filters.before (ISO dates); search and news sections merge, deduplicated by URL; HTML markup stripped from titles and snippets |
| octen | Octen POST /search | query = query text clipped to 500 chars, count=k, language=[en], safesearch=off, highlight.enable=true with max_tokens=512; site: → include_domains, after:/before: → start_time/end_time (full-day UTC bounds); highlight is the snippet |
| jina | Jina GET s.jina.ai | q = full query with site:/after:/before: kept as Google operators, num=min(k,20); default mode fetches each result page, so the snippet is the page markdown (content) clipped client-side to the 2,000-char budget, falling back to the SERP description when the fetch returns nothing; published date from publishedTime, else the SERP date string |
| context.dev | Context.dev POST /v1/web/search | query = query text clipped to 500 chars, numResults=min(max(k,10),100) (API floor is 10), country=us, queryFanout=false; site: → includeDomains, after: (with no before:) → freshness last_24_hours/last_week/last_month/last_year covering that date, before: dropped; snippet is the SERP description, markdown scrape left off |
| deepseek | DeepSeek POST /v1/tools/search | query = query text clipped to fit 128 chars with site: (several joined by OR), lang:en and after:/before: as native operators, count=min(k,50), need_content=false; snippet is the long query-aware summary, falling back to the short snippet; published date from date |
| claude-search | Anthropic POST /v1/messages | model=claude-sonnet-5, max_tokens=32000, tools=[web_search_20250305 with max_uses=1], system prompt asks for a JSON array of every search result with the snippet copied verbatim (≤500 chars); rank = web_search_tool_result order, title/snippet from the JSON row with the same URL, published_date = page_age; the prompt tells the model to pass the query to the tool verbatim (operators kept as text), site: also → allowed_domains |
| chatgpt-search | OpenAI POST /v1/responses | model=gpt-5.5, reasoning.effort=low, max_output_tokens=32000, tools=[web_search with search_context_size=low], tool_choice forces the search, include=web_search_call.action.sources, same system prompt; rank = action.sources order, title/snippet from the JSON row with the same URL (utm_ parameters stripped); the prompt tells the model to pass the query to the tool verbatim (operators kept as text), site: also → filters.allowed_domains |

### Appendix G — raw artifacts

everything you need to re-score a run from scratch

| artifact | contents | where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| per-run reports | every query and result (title, URL, snippet), judge rating + penalized rating + reasoning, per-call latencies, and the run's config (judge_model, num_results) | [HF: keenable-ai/needle-results](https://huggingface.co/datasets/keenable-ai/needle-results), runs/<run-id>/ |
| query files | the exact queries each run issued, including gold answers (fresh.jsonl, gold.jsonl, scholar.jsonl, legal.jsonl) | same run folder |
| dashboard aggregates | history.jsonl, overlap.jsonl, uniqueness.jsonl, independence.json, runs.json, ci_scores.jsonl, ci.json | data/ on this site (gh-pages) |
| prompts & scoring code | judge prompt (shared/prompts/judgement.jinja), metric implementations, engine clients | [GitHub: keenableai/needle](https://github.com/keenableai/needle) |
